Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026

How do we justify our view? 
Growth outlook Valuation Earnings revisions

Growth outlook

VIS: quarterly EPS growth trend 
VIS’s earnings have been volatile due to the cyclical nature 
of the semiconductor industry in which it operates. That 
said, barring its hefty growth during 2021-22 as an outlier 
due to the COVID-inflated chip demand, VIS will enjoy 30-
70% YoY EPS growth over 2Q26E-4Q27E, according to 
our forecasts, with strength similar to that in 2016 and 
2018. On an annual EPS basis, we forecast a 30% CAGR 
over 2025-28.

Source: Company, Daiwa estimates and forecasts

Valuation   VIS: 4-quarter-forward PER bands 
Trading at 24x on our 4-quarter-forward EPS, the VIS stock 
is priced at the high end of its post-2009 GFC range of 5-
30x, suggesting a not-so-compelling valuation vs. history. 
Yet, considering our forecast of a robust 30% EPS CAGR 
over 2025-28, 24x does not look demanding from a PEG 
point of view. Valued by our ROE-adjusted PBR target of 
5.2x, our 12M TP of TWD210 for VIS translates into a 26x 
4-quarter forward PER, which looks reasonable in 
combination of both PBR and PER methodologies.

Source: Company, TEJ, Daiwa estimates and forecasts

Earnings revision

VIS: consensus EPS forecast revisions 
Bloomberg consensus 2026-27 EPS forecasts for VIS have 
been trending up since February 2026, indicating positive 
investor sentiment. Our 2027E EPS is 5% above 
consensus, implying room for further upward revisions from 
the market to act as an upside catalyst for the stock, in our 
opinion.

Source: Bloomberg

Company profile  
Founded in December 1994 and listed in March 1998, Vanguard International Semiconductor Corp. 
(VIS) successfully transformed its business model from memory to a complementary metal-oxide-
semiconductor (CMOS) logic foundry focused on specialty technologies such as mixed-signal, power 
analog and embedded memory, becoming one of the world’s leading specialty foundries. Leveraging its 
strategic relationship with major shareholder TSMC, VIS operates wafer fabs in Taiwan and Singapore, 
serving a wide variety of customers, including fabless chipmakers and IDMs globally.

### Page 5

32

Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026 
Fast ramp to capture AI ripple effect 
We initiate coverage of VIS with an Outperform (2) rating and 12M TP of TWD210. 
Founded in December 1994, headquartered in Hsinchu Science Park and listed in March 
1998 (see Appendix 1 for more details on the company), VIS looks set to enjoy fully loaded 
operations (in terms of wafer-in) through 2027, with sustained pricing power, thanks to the 
ripple effect of AI demand scaling from compute to connectivity and power, further 
augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation.

Given its longstanding ties with TSMC, which currently holds a c.19% stake in VIS and has 
strategically helped it advance its business transformation by moving up value verticals of 
specialty-foundry offerings to differentiate itself in the competitive specialty foundry market, 
VIS looks set to benefit from this multi-year AI scaling, among peers. The fast expansion of 
its first 12” fab in Singapore should help cement the AI business and capitalise on what we 
observe as a paradigm shift in the foundry industry, as mentioned in the main section of 
this report.

Fully loaded operations on AI ripple effect 
Like many other semiconductor foundries, VIS enjoyed unusual pricing power during the 
COVID period as the virus, together with geopolitical tensions between China and the US, 
inflated chip demand across virtually all product applications to an unprecedented level. 
Consequently, the industry suffered from an unprecedented 2-year downturn on a 
prolonged inventory correction, if excluding the AI build that has helped rescue the 
advanced foundry segment, save the village (ie, the chip industry) and bring the global chip 
cycle back on its historical track of an average of a one-year downturn. This downturn 
caused choppy operations for many second-tier foundries which focused initially on the 
non-AI space over 2022-25, including VIS (see our series of Technology Outlook reports: 
2023: V-shape, U-shape or double dip?, 2024: The dragon is ready to soar, 2025: likely 
longer and stronger).

Starting from 2H25, after the AI builds scaled out and across from compute into memory to 
connectivity and power, creating a ripple effect in demand that benefits not only advanced 
but also specialty foundries, we expect VIS’s wafer loadings to approach full levels through 
2027 (in terms of wafer-in), despite its fast 12” expansion. With its parent company TSMC’s 
strategic partnership, VIS stands at a strong position to benefit from this multi-year AI 
scaling, in our view. To maximise the AI business potential, VIS is accelerating its first 12” 
fab in Singapore via a JV named VisionPower Semiconductor Manufacturing Corp 
(VSMC), with phase-one investment of USD6.8bn where, in terms of registered capital of 
USD4bn, VIS assumes USD2.4bn for a 60% stake, while NXP assumes USD1.6bn for a 
40% stake.

In terms of the total investment on a cash basis, VIS will chip in only USD1.0bn, with 
USD2.8bn coming from NXP and USD1.1bn from TSMC with some equipment 
consignment, while the rest will come from other customer prepayments, loans and a 
subsidy from the Singapore government. Upon VSMC’s completion, this will bring VIS a 
total of 6 fabs, with combined capacity reaching up to c.380,000 8”-equivalent wafers per 
month (wpm) by end-2028, based on our forecasts (see table on next page for details of 
our key business assumptions).

Fast expansion to capture the paradigm shift 
On top of the AI scaling creating a ripple effect for demand spillover, such strength looks to 
be further augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation, as assessed in the main section of this 
report. To capitalise on the likely paradigm shift in the specialty foundry segment by 
TSMC’s fab optimisation, VIS is pulling in its first 12” fab construction around half a year 
ahead of schedule, aiming to start mass production from 1Q27 after it hosted an opening 
ceremony on 28 September.

Registered under VSMC, we expect this fab to reach its first-phase designed capacity of 
44,000 wpm by end-2028, subject to market dynamics. c.30% of the fab’s capacity will be 
dedicated to TSMC’s wafer production of silicon interposers handling chip-on-wafer-on-
substrate (CoWoS) advanced packaging, which has seen meaningful supply constraint 
due to strong AI high-performance compute (HPC) demand. The rest of the capacity is for 
other customers’ power semiconductors (such as mixed-signal, analog and PMIC) and 
embedded flash, focusing on AI scaling and automotive and industrial (A&I) applications.

Despite the 12” expansion pull-in, we do not expect the ramp to dwarf capacity utilisations 
since all the planned capacity is already sold out, thanks to the long-term agreements 
(LTA) signed with customers amid the supply constraints, which should help VIS manage 
surging depreciation and minimise margin dilution for financial resilience. Although this 12” 
fab has little to do with TSMC’s 28/22/20nm fab optimisation addressed in the main 
section, since it covers only process nodes ranging from 0.13u to 40nm and thus enjoys 
only the 65/55nm wafer interposer spillover from TSMC, on our market research, we see 
possibilities for TSMC to shut down more legacy 12” fabs in the future. We believe this may 
incentivise VIS to speed up its second 12” fab construction in Singapore, namely the 
phase-two, or VSMC Fab 2, for 40,000-50,000 wpm capacity beyond 2028 and is scalable 
to 28nm-and-below and even the finFET (FF) process nodes.

Financial forecasts 
In the near term, we expect VIS to enjoy counter-seasonal quarters through 2027, thanks 
to the ripple effect of AI scaling on wafer loadings and pricing power. We estimate that VIS 
earned 3Q26 EPS of TWD1.57, in line with the Bloomberg consensus estimate. For 4Q26, 
we are behind consensus since 1) we expect a diminished effect of price hikes after our 
estimated 2-3% QoQ EPS increases in 3Q26; and 2) we do not expect the 12” contribution 
to ramp that soon. In the long term, we forecast VIS to deliver a revenue CAGR of nearly 
30% over 2026-28 for an EPS CAGR of 26%.

Revenue and customers 
As of 2Q26, power management IC (PMIC) accounted for the majority of revenue (77%; 
see chart on next page), on a rising trend from 40% a decade ago thanks to the co-efforts 
between VIS and TSMC strategically transforming the former’s business up the value 
verticals from logic commodity display drivers (D/D) for consumer electronics devices to 
power discrete, analog, and PMIC for not only consumer but A&I applications. Riding on 
the escalating trend of demand for advanced high-V PMIC accommodating AI servers and 
power infrastructure under the AI cycle, we expect the PMIC revenue contribution to rise 
further over our forecast period, while the D/D contribution will drop to c.10%-plus for base-
line customer demand support.

As of 2Q26, VIS found 16%, 16%, and 46% of revenue from major process nodes of 
0.25u, 0.18u, and 0.13u-and-below, respectively, on our estimates. Since the VSMC fab 
will focus on 0.13u all the way to 40nm, and TSMC’s wafer interposers will take c.30% of 
the capacity ramp on our estimates focusing on 65/55nm, we expect the 0.13u-and-below 
to increase to more than 60% of revenue over our forecast period. Both the PMIC and 
node advances should be blended ASP and margin accretive, helping mitigate the 
depreciation burden associated with the VSMC fab ramp. Illustrated in a chart on the next 
page, we expect VIS’s blended ASP to trend upward from c.USD630 in 2Q26, rising by 1-
5% QoQ through our forecast period to exceed USD700 by 2028.

VIS services a high variety of customers around the world, including most fabless 
chipmakers in Taiwan and mainland China focusing on the D/D, power MOSFET and 
PMIC, and most integrated device manufacturers (IDM) across Japan, Europe and the US 
focusing on power semiconductor foundry works.

Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026 
Cost analysis 
Over the past decades, VIS’s cost structure has fluctuated in fixed overheads (mainly D&A) 
from time to time, depending on its fab acquisitions, varying from 10% to 30% of COGS 
(refer back to the key assumption table). Given VSMC is about to ramp in production with a 
total capex of USD6.8bn for phase one, or roughly 2x VIS’s 2025-26E aggregate revenue, 
we expect the D&A portion to return to the 30% level over our forecast period, acting as a 
margin dilutor. Yet with VIS’s capex budget of TWD60-70bn (c.USD2bn) this year, we 
expect the capex to largely subside from 2027 for the depreciation cycle to peak in 2029. 
As a result, we forecast its D&A in absolute terms to rise by 54% YoY in 2027 to TWD14bn 
and 33% YoY in 2028 to nearly TWD19bn, with gross margin (GM) stalling at 32-34% over 
2Q26-1Q28, then picking up to 35% or higher in 2H28 and likely rising further after the 
depreciation comes off the cycle into 2029.

Minority interest 
Minority interest (MI), or non-controlling interest as stated by VIS, kicked in from 1Q25 and 
rose to 2.4% of pretax income in 2Q26 due to preparation for the VSMC investment. We 
forecast the MI to rise to TWD341m this year (TWD137m in 2025), TWD568m in 2027 and 
TWD1.45bn in 2028, mainly from the 40% MI owned by NXP as VIS ramps the 12” fab 
investment in Singapore. As illustrated in a table below, we forecast VSMC to record 
revenue of nearly TWD9bn in 2027 and TWD26bn in 2028 for operating loss of TWD1.4bn 
and operating profit of TWD3.6bn, respectively, on the basis of a standalone operating 
entity.

Dividend policy 
Over the past decade, VIS has stepped up its dividend payout ratios from 50% in 2011 to 
more than 100% this year. We expect the company to keep the ratios at c.80% over our 
forecast period, subject to the AI development which should determine the capex 
requirement for the VSMC investment, affecting its FCF generating power. VIS paid a 
TWD4.5/share cash dividend this year (against last year’s earnings), and we forecast it to 
pay TWD5.0/share for 2027 with a payout ratio of 85% against this year’s earnings for a 
2.7% yield based on the closing price as of 5 October.

VIS: free cashflow trend     VIS: debt-to-equity ratio

Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts  Source: Company, Daiwa forecasts

Cashflow and gearing 
Given its hefty capex over 2025-27E, we expect VIS’s free cashflow (FCF) to turn negative 
during the same period. Foreseeing the cash gap, VIS issued new shares of 228m in 2024 
for a 12% per-share dilution and collected TWD17.6bn. It further issued ECBs in February 
this year to collect nearly TWD11bn; therefore, we forecast its debt-to-equity (D/E) ratio to 
approach 170% in 2026. Yet with the VSMC ramp in profitability for operating cash inflow, 
we forecast the FCF to turn positive in 2028, helping lower the gearing to c.140%.

Valuation and risk 
We employ an ROE-adjusted PBR methodology to value the foundry stocks under our 
coverage, where we compare a foundry’s projected ROE with its historical ROE average, 
work out a premium or discount, and apply it to the foundry’s historical average PBR for 
our ROE-adjusted PBR target. Given TSMC is VIS’s strategic partner, we tweak our 
methodology a bit by anchoring VIS’s ROE-adjusted PBR to TSMC’s, where we compare 
their long-term ROE projections (2004-28E), work out a discount of 33% and then apply 
the discount to our 7.7x target PBR for TSMC, resulting in our target PBR of 5.2x and 12M 
TP of TWD210 for VIS on our one-year forward BVPS forecast.

Key downside risks to our call would be: 1) the current chip upcycle ends earlier than 
expected; and 2) VSMC’s 12” fab ramp/execution comes in below expectation.

Vanguard International Semiconductor (5347 TT): 9 October 2026 
Appendix: company profile 
Founded in December 1994 with headquarters located in Hsinchu Science Park and 
shares listed in March 1998, Vanguard International Semiconductor (VIS) is a leading 
specialty foundry in the world focusing on mix signal, power semiconductor and embedded 
memory chip manufacturing for value-added differentiation against competition in the 
mature foundry market. With strategic help from its parent company TSMC on business 
transformation and technology transfer, VIS commanded a 1.1% share in the global 
foundry market by 2025 revenue on our estimates (total size c.USD140bn), or nearly 4% in 
the global mature foundry market (ex-TSMC as a proxy), behind SMIC, UMC, 
GlobalFoundries (GF) and Huahong Grace Semiconductor (HHGrace; see chart on the 
next page). Note: our foundry market calculation is for dedicated foundry works only, 
different from TSMC’s revised definition of the Foundry 2.0 that includes not only the 
dedicated foundry but packaging, testing and other non-memory semiconductor 
manufacturing services.

The foundry market 
Ever since TSMC’s founder innovated the semiconductor foundry service model, the 
foundry market has been in operation for more than 3 decades. Thanks to a rise in the 
global AI buildout, the global foundry revenue, after a dip in 2023 on the post-COVID 
inventory correction, recovered by more than 20% YoY pa over 2024-25. We forecast the 
growth to accelerate to more than 30% YoY this year, with revenue reaching USD186bn 
and exceeding USD255bn in 2028 for a robust 23% CAGR over 2025-28. If excluding 
TSMC, we forecast the market to grow by 14% YoY this year and approach USD60bn in 
2028 for a CAGR of 13%, where we expect VIS to outgrow the market growth over our 
forecast period as illustrated in a chart below, thanks to its fast capacity expansion to 
capitalise on the AI scaling and paradigm shift in the specialty foundry segment.

Operation and customer 
VIS currently operates 5 wafer fabs with 4 in Taiwan, including Fab 1 which commenced 
operations in 1994 as a memory setup and then converted to mixed signal, power metal 
oxide semiconductor field effect transistor (MOSFET) and logic foundry fab; Fab 2 which 
was acquired in 2007 from Winbond; Fab 3 which was acquired from Nanya Tech in 2014; 
and Fab 5 which was acquired from AUO in 2021, plus 1 in Singapore, Fab VS1, acquired 
from GF in 2019 and previously named 3E. The 5 fabs currently operate a total of 280,000 
wpm capacity all in 8” wafer size, in which the Fab VS1 and 5 are scalable for an extra 
10,000 wpm capacity each, though VIS doesn’t seem to be keen in exercising the 
scalability since VSMC apparently is the focus for expansion. Following its opening 
ceremony on 28 September this year, VIS’s first 12” fab via VSMC is slated for mass-
production from 1Q27, aimed to bring up total capacity to c.380,000 wpm in 8” equivalent 
wafer by end-2028.

 Source: Bloomberg

VIS doesn’t disclose its key customers due to confidentiality issues, but we believe its key 
customers cover most of the fabless chipmakers in Taiwan and mainland China focusing 
on display driver (D/D), power MOSFET and power management IC (PMIC), as well as 
integrated device manufacturers (IDM) across Japan, Europe and the US. According to our 
industry survey, key D/D vendors in the greater China region are Novatek (3034 TT, 
TWD529, Hold [3]), Himax (HIMX US, not rated), Raydium (3592 TT, not rated), Ilitek (6962 
TT, not rated), Sitronix Technology (8016 TT, not rated), FocalTech (3545 TT, not rated), 
Chipone Technology (not listed), OmniVision (603501 CH, CNY82.21, Hold [3]) and 
GalaxyCore (688728 CH, not rated), while key power semiconductor vendors in the world 
include Qualcomm (QCOM US, USD177.12, Outperform [2]; covered by Louis Miscioscia), 
Renesas (6723 JP, JPY3,924, Outperform [2]; covered by Junji Okawa), Monolithic Power 
Systems (MPS; MPWR US, not rated), NXP (NXPI US, not rated), STMicro (STM US, not 
rated), Infineon (IFX GR, not rated), Microchip (MCHP US, not rated), TI (TXN US, not 
rated), MediaTek (2454 TT, TWD4,835, Buy [1], via Richtek), Global Mixed-mode 
Technology (GMT; 8081 TT, not rated) and Niko Semiconductor (3317 TT, not rated).

Shareholder structure 
VIS’s major shareholders were TSMC and the Taiwan government’s Development Funds, 
with c.19% and c.16% of holdings, respectively, as of August 2026 under total share 
outstanding of 1.88bn. With management in aggregate holding an insignificant stake and 
the rest being held by institutional investors (QFII holding was 32.7% as of 5 October), we 
estimate a healthy free float of more than 60% for the stock, with decent cap of c.USD11bn 
and 3-month daily turnover of more than USD100m.

TSMC reduced its holding in VIS this year by 8pp, raising investor concerns on whether 
the move would jeopardise the former’s strategic support to the latter. We see no direct link 
between the stake reduction and strategic support, and believe TSMC will continue the 
support, evidenced by its licence of gallium-nitride (GaN) and interposer technologies and 
equipment consignment to VSMC.

ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 Oct 2026 
Source:  Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
VIS's board of directors (BoD) consists of 9 members, including 5 independent directors,
representing 56% of the total board seats. The board comprises members with diverse
backgrounds in business management, finance, industry, and academia, providing a broad
range of expertise and perspectives.
We believe the majority-independent board structure and the directors’ collective experience in
leadership, corporate governance, industry expertise, financial management, risk management,
and sustainability strengthen the effectiveness of the BoD and support VIS's long-term growth
and corporate governance practices.
Capital management 1 VIS pays its dividend on a quarterly basis. In the past 5 years, it has managed its dividendpayout well, as evidenced by its dividend in dollar terms, where it has never lowered its dividend.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were around 1-2% of its total revenue each year. The percentage isinsignificant and thus we see limited risk for VIS.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
VIS has established a comprehensive sustainable supply chain management framework to
ensure suppliers comply with its sustainability requirements covering environmental protection,
labor and human rights, occupational health and safety, business ethics, and responsible
sourcing. The company requires suppliers to adhere to its "VIS Supplier Code of Conduct" and
"Corporate Sustainability Policy", and conducts supplier sustainability assessments and audits
as part of its supplier management process. We believe these efforts strengthen VIS's supply
chain resilience and help mitigate supply chain risks.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
VIS is committed to improving water efficiency and sustainable water management. In 2022, the
company implemented the ISO 46001 Water Efficiency Management System to analyze water
consumption patterns, identify significant water-use equipment, evaluate water-saving
opportunities, and establish improvement measures. VIS also provides annual water
management training at its Taiwan facilities to promote continuous improvement in water-use
efficiency. We believe these efforts demonstrate the company's commitment to responsible
water stewardship and help reduce operational risks associated with water resources.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
VIS adopts a comprehensive resource management approach to waste management and has
established waste management procedures in accordance with ISO 14001 standards. Through
dedicated waste management personnel and regular employee training, the company promotes
effective waste reduction, classification, collection, storage, and treatment practices across its
operations.
E GHG emissions 1
VIS adopts a source-segregation and multi-stage treatment approach, supported by high-
efficiency air pollution control systems, to effectively manage VOC and acidic/alkaline gas
emissions from semiconductor manufacturing processes.
The company's emission levels remain well below regulatory limits, reflecting its commitment to
environmental compliance and sustainable operations.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

Unimicron

Extending capacity plan for ABF substrate 
and better margins in PCB/HDI; Buy

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 1,650 TWD | Price: 1,270 TWD

Raise PO along with the hike in estimates 
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 5%/12%/10% after baking in a strengthened margin 
profile, partly thanks to further improved GM (gross margin) in PCB (printed circuit 
board) and HDI (high density interconnect) business. Our PO goes to NT$1,650 (was 
NT$1,400) same based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. Reiterate Buy on Unimicron in view of 
its structurally growing exposure to AI-related (artificial intelligence) products, which in 
turn bolsters its GM profile in longer-term. 
Land acquisition to back long-term capacity growth  
Unimicron announced that it bought a land/building located in Hukuo (Hsinchu) from 
Bridgestone Taiwan, with the amount of NT$10.1bn. Our check with the company 
suggests that such property will be used for further capacity expansion beyond its YM 
(Yangmei) site, but the timing for output will only take place in 2030 at earliest. Of note, 
the company will expand 30+% of its ABF (Ajinomoto build-up film) capacity in 2027 via 
the KF2 (KuangFu 2) plant, and there will be YM3 capacity coming on stream in 2029 
after the expansion YM2 in 2028 backed by 5-year LTA (long-term agreement). For 
2027/28 capex, currently we expect to reach NT$40+bn each year. 
Rosier margin outlook in PCB/HDI business  
Unimicron is one of the major (if not biggest) PCB suppliers to high-end (see report) 
optical module, where demand growth is expected to remain robust coupled with spec 
upgrades in the upcoming years. Management cites such business carries 30+% GM 
currently, while the number by AI HDI in China production lines is moving closer to peers’ 
level. Yet, the PCB/HDI lines in Taiwan still carry lower GM. Currently, we assume its GM 
in HDI business to reach 20+% from mid-2027, along with the continuous yield/output 
improvements.

Company Sector 
Electronics

Company Description 
Unimicron (3037 TT) was founded in 1990 and listed on 
the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 1998. The firm manufactures 
a wide range of PCB and IC substrates for consumer 
electronics, industrial, and automotive. It is also the leading 
ABF supplier globally. The company merged with Phoenix 
Precision Technology (PPT, delisted) in 2009 and expanded 
its exposure in the IC substrate business. UMC is the 
biggest shareholder in Unimicron with a 13.3% stake.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Unimicron in view of strong 
demand from AI accelerators (especially ASICs), which we 
expect to boost the utilization rate of the ABF substrate 
business from 2H26, in turn bolstering the company's 
margin profile and profitability going into 2027.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
Unimicron (UMCRF) 
Our PO of NT$1,650 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E, which is roughly 1 standard deviation 
above the historical average of 20x since 2017. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, 
as we expect Unimicron's earnings to be supported by its high-end technology offerings, 
where end market demand seems more resilient.

Upside risks are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) greater PC and regular server demand, and (3) 
lower competition pressure.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) lower ASP content increase 
at AI server, and (3) bigger drop in the demand of consumer electronic products.

### Page 2

Earnings revision: We factor in WNC 2Q26 result and revise up earnings by 1%/ 2%/ 2% 
in 2026-28E to reflect higher than expected revenues of WiFi 7/ WiFi 8 and switch 
products, driven by spec upgrade and rising client demand. We keep GM and Opex ratio 
largely unchanged.

Valuation: With the earning estimate changes, we raise our 12M TP to NT$380.0 (vs. 
previously at NT$371, based on 28.0x 2027E P/E, unchanged). We continue to base 
our TP on 2027E P/E, and the target multiple is still derived from the correlation 
between P/E and EPS growth of its peers, which is higher as we mark to market. Our 
target P/E of 28.0x is higher than company’s avg. +1 std P/E (16.3x), which we believe is 
not stretched considering the sustainable growth of WNC driven by long-term satellite 
and high speed networking demand. Maintain Buy.

Price Target Risks and Methodology: WNC 
Valuation: We use a near-term P/E to derive our 12m target price for WNC, consistent 
with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our TP of NT$380.0 on a target P/E 
multiple of 28.0x on a forward year EPS (2027E). Our target P/E is derived from the 
correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers. We are Buy rated on WNC. Key 
downside risks: fiercer-than-expected competition in satellite communication, 
slower-than-expected Wi-Fi 7 or 5G FWA ramp-up in US and Europe markets.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

嘉澤：全球前三大 CPU Socket 供應商，伺服器已成營收主軸
全球前三大中央處理器插座CPU socket供應商。依Bishop統計在電腦暨週邊連
接器廠商中全球排名第四，不分行業別的全球連接器排名全球排名第十八。
主要客戶為AMD與Intel。產品涵蓋 CPU Socket、記憶體插槽、高速連接器及
線材，並延伸至 SOCAMM、液冷快接頭與 NPO Socket。
本研究預估，嘉澤伺服器營收由 2026 年221.14 億元增至 2027 年 328.67 億元，
年增 48.6%。成長來自板量回升、CPU新平台提高 Socket 與高速連接器價值，
以及 AI 新產品放量；PC 相關營收占比則由 2025 年 36.60%，降至 2027 年
16.5%，營運重心持續轉向伺服器。

CPU socket是甚麼
CPU 插槽的設計目的是將 CPU 處理器牢固地固定在主機板上，並在設備移動時
有助於防止潛在的損壞。嘉澤主要客戶為AMD與Intel。
資料來源 :premioinc.com
PGA (插針網格陣列)BGA (球柵陣列)LGA (平面網格陣列)項目
針腳在CPU上，插入主
機板的孔位中。
CPU 底部是微小錫球，直
接高溫焊接在主機板上。
CPU 底部是平貼金屬觸點，
針腳做在主機板插槽上。外觀結構
可以。直接拉起拉桿即
可拔插更換。
不行。屬於永久焊接，需
專業 BGA 焊台設備才能拆
卸。
可以。可自由解鎖扣具、更
換或升級 CPU。更換/升級
1. 針腳在 CPU 上，輕
微彎曲時通常還能手動
扳正修復。
1. 體積最薄、最省空間。
適合行動裝置。
1.風險轉移：安裝失誤時損
壞的是主機板，CPU 價值
較高(倍數差距)

代理 AI 讓 CPU 工作量增加：從回覆問題，走向執行任務
原先：用戶提出問題；應用程式向模型發送提示；模型生成答案；應用程式將答
案傳回給用戶。主節點CPU負責調度、I/O和系統管理，而GPU則負責繁重的運
算任務。
現在：代理AI不再只是回答一個問題，而是將目標分解成多個步驟，決定下一步
該做什麼，調用多個模型，查詢資料庫，連接API，運行企業應用程序，檢查權
限，檢索內存，驗證輸出，然後再次循環執行整個過程。
CPU負責：編排：管理分解複雜任務的引擎。代理執行和工具呼叫：觸發 API 和
傳統企業軟體。策略與安全：對每個自主操作進行實際檢查。
隨 AI 從模型推論延伸至 Agent 執行，CPU 的角色擴及任務協調、工具操作及資
料處理。AMD 5 月官方文章已描述 CPU：GPU 由過去約 1：4–8，朝 1：1 移
動；Intel Q2 法說進一步表示，目前以顆數計已接近均衡。兩家原廠的觀察，支
持 CPU 在 AI 基礎建設中重要性提高

資料來源 :AMD官網(智能體人工智慧改變了CPU/GPU的平衡)
5

作」的分散式架構。未來不是只增加 GPU，而是新增獨立的 Agentic CPU
compute layer
New Agentic CPU Racks：新增的 Agent 執行層負責 Agent Control
Plane 與 Tool Execution管理 agent 的任務拆解、工作流調度、狀態管理與
多 agent 協作執行 API 呼叫、SQL 查詢、文件檢索、資料處理、程式執行與

能是把 LLM 的「決策」轉換成企業系統中的「實際行動」

Meta Muse：個人代理產品化，使 CPU 執行環境的需求更具體
Muse 的投資意義在於：大型消費平台開始提供可持續執行任務的個人代理，使
CPU 沙盒需求從技術架構走向實際服務。
與AMD的關係Meta已部署數百萬顆AMD EPYC，並是Venice、Verano 的領先
客戶。因此，Meta 的運算擴張是 AMD CPU 需求的重要觀察對象。
持續在線的虛擬機（Persistent VMs）： Meta Muse 的底層採用了 Muse
Secure VM 架構。這些 VM 即使在使用者沒有主動點開 App 時，也在後台持續
運作，消耗大量的作業系統配置、記憶體管理與安全驗證資源—這些全部屬於通
用運算，只有 CPU 能做。因此當億萬名用戶的 AI 代理同時在線，CPU 的短缺
會遠比 GPU 來得更強烈。Muse 這樣的個人代理規模不斷擴大，對 CPU 的需求
勢必會增長，預計 CPU 與 GPU 的比例將從 1:1往4:1以上走 (intel電話會議)

資料來源 :trendforce, meta

化

開發NPO socket，為未來潛在商機
資料來源 :moneyDJ、利成光電科技有限公司
圖片來源：OIF 的《Co-Packaging Framework》第 10 頁、圖 2(d) 21
NPO的核心定義是：將光引擎從傳統的前面板可插拔位置，移動到更靠近交
換晶片（ASIC） 的地方，但光引擎和ASIC仍然是兩個獨立的封裝體。它們通
常被並排安裝在同一塊高性能基板（如矽中介層或有機基板）上
NPO Socket已進入客戶最後認證階段，且達可量產狀態，量產目標落在2027
年第3季，目前未納入估計，等待未來公司給更多指引。
AI資料中心在Scale-up與Scale-out兩種架構同步拉高頻寬需求，嘉澤在NPO、
既有高速銅傳輸與高功率連接方案的產品組合，將對應不同平台導入時程。

9 月经济数据点评：生产偏强、需求渐进修复

[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

Email:weizheng@lczq.com Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com  
证书:S1320524100001 证书:S1320523020004

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
6.2%升至 9.8%，我们理解部分原因是地缘局势提高能源安全和保供要
求，国内油气增储上产持续推进，为采矿业生产提供了支撑 。二是电热燃
水业增速回升。电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增加值增长 5.6%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点。从实物产量看，核电、风电和太阳能发电分别
增长 21.5%、10.6%和 19.5%。 
服务业生产明显改善，生产性服务业延续支撑，生活性服务有修复。9
月，服务业生产指数同比增长 4.8%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点；一是数
字信息和生产配套型服务业继续保持较快增长。 信息传输软件和信息技术
服务业、租赁和商务服务业、交通运输仓储和邮政业生产指数 分别增长
9.8%、9.5%和 5.4%。二是服务业改善广度有提升。住宿和餐饮业生产指
数增长 5.3%，服务业商务活动指数由 49.3%升至 50.2%，重新进入扩张
区间；电信广播电视及卫星传输、货币金融和保险等行业商务活动指数均
位于 55%以上1。 
投资低位企稳。9 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 7.2%，降幅与 8 月持
平。按累计金额反推，9 月当月投资同比约下降 7.2%，较 8 月的下降
11.0%明显收窄；剔除房地产后，当月投资同比约下降 3.4%，降幅较 8
月收窄约 4.4 个百分点。季调环比增长 0.07%，是 3 月以来首次转正。 
制造业投资小幅修复，增长仍集中在少数优势领域 。9 月，制造业累计投
资下降 2.1%，降幅较 1—8 月收窄 0.2 个百分点。结构上看，一是正增行
业仍主要集中在装备和新兴制造领域。国家统计局公布的 13 个制造业行
业中，只有 5 个保持正增长，其中铁路船舶航空航天、计算机通信电子设
备制造业投资分别增长 15.8%和 9.6%，纺织、电气机械和通用设备制造
业分别增长 4.2%、1.2%和 1.1%。高技术产业投资增长 4.9%，其中信息
服务、航空航天器及设备、电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长 20.2%、
12.5%和 7.6%。二是上游原材料和下游消费制造业投资仍较弱。农副食
品加工、食品制造、化工、医药、有色金属冶炼、金属制品、专用设备和
汽车制造业投资分别下降 2.5%、10.3%、7.5%、8.1%、5.1%、10.1%、
8.9%和 6.3%。 
地产继续承压，价格端修复难抵销售走弱。9 月，地产投资累计同比下降
20.6%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.7 个百分点；按累计金额反推，9 月当月投资
下降约 26.3%，降幅较 8 月扩大约 0.8 个百分点。结构上看，一是地产销
售再次走弱，约束投资预期。 商品房销售面积和销售额累计分别下降
13.2%和 13.9%，降幅较 1—8 月扩大 1.1 和 0.9 个百分点；按累计金额反
推，9 月当月销售面积和销售额分别下降约 20.7%和 20.1%，较 8 月的下
降 14.8%和 12.1%明显走弱。东部地区销售额降幅由 12.6%扩大至
13.4%。30 大中城市商品房成交面积同比下降 10.9%，较 8 月扩大约 7.5
个百分点，其中一线城市增速由 18.8%降至 2.4%，二线城市降幅由
15.8%扩大至 22.7%，三线城市降幅基本持平。二是供给和资金端仍然偏
弱。新开工面积累计下降 24.9%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.1 个百分点，竣工面
积降幅由 23.7%扩大至 25.1%。房企到位资金下降 21.6%，降幅扩大 0.6

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
个百分点，其中，自筹资金降幅由 20.0%扩大至 21.4%；国内贷款和个人
按揭贷款降幅则分别小幅收窄至 33.1%和 22.1%。三是价格端有积极变
化。9 月，70 城新房和二手房价格同比分别下降 3.0%和 4.8%，降幅均较
8 月收窄 0.3 个百分点；环比看，新房降幅由 0.2%收窄至 0.1%，二手房
下降 0.3%，与上月持平。一线城市新房和二手房价格分别上涨 0.1%和
0.2%，二线城市新房价格由下降转为持平、二手房降幅收窄。 
基建投资继续放缓，项目端景气 有改善迹象。9 月，基建投资累计同比下
降 4.2%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.2 个百分点。一方面，部分传统基建领域仍
然偏弱。交通运输仓储和邮政业投资增长 1.9%，较前期回落 0.2 个百分
点；水利环境和公共设施管理业投资下降 12.8%，降幅扩大 1.8 个百分
点，其中水利管理和公共设施管理业均下降 12%以上。电热燃水投资下降
6.8%，降幅较前期小幅收窄。另一方面，项目储备和部分结构性领域出现
积极信号，但向施工和实物工作量的传导仍不充分。9 月，建筑业商务活
动指数由 46.9%升至 50.3%，从业人员和业务活动预期指数也有所提高。
信息传输、航空运输和水上运输投资分别增长 30.8%、13.8%和 13.5%，
交通、能源、数据等“六张网”相关领域仍有支撑。 建筑业新订单指数由
42.4%升至 45.7%，尚未回到扩张区间，指向前端项目需求有改善，但向
施工和实物工作量的传导仍不充分。 
消费是内需端改善最明显的分项，商品消费仍受汽车拖累 。9 月，社零同
比增长 1.1%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.35%，剔除汽车后
社零同比增长 3.9%，较 8 月加快 1.4 个百分点。结构上看，商品和餐饮
消费同步恢复。9 月，商品零售额增长 1.0%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点；
餐饮收入增长 2.1%，提高 1.0 个百分点。从更广义的消费口径看，服务
消费仍是消费的重要支撑。三季度，社会消费商品和服务零售总额增长
2.6%，其中服务零售额增长 5.0%，明显高于商品零售额的 1.0%；网上
服务零售额增长 5.3%。通讯信息、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务零售额
保持较快增长。商品消费方面，必选消费和通讯器材维持韧性，汽车是主
要拖累。服装、化妆品、日用品、家电和中西药品零售额分别增长
1.2%、5.4%、4.1%、1.9%和 5.0%，通讯器材类增长 31.1%。汽车零售
额下降 21.2%，降幅较 8 月扩大 2.7 个百分点；家具、建筑装潢材料仍分
别下降 3.0%和 9.2%，降幅较 8 月有所收窄。 
展望后续，四季度经济有望延续生产较强、需求渐进修复的格局，未来增
量支撑主要来自两条线索。总体看，三季度实际增长边际企稳，优势制造
业、外需和服务消费构成主要支撑。往后看，增量支撑线索有两方面。一
方面，以“六张网”和 8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具为抓手，加快资金
投放和项目开工。9 月以来三家政策性银行投放的新型政策性金融工具资
金已经超过 1000 亿元2，建筑业 PMI 和新订单指数同步回升，指向政策
正在向项目端传导。不过，从补充资本金、落实配套融资到形成施工和实
物工作量仍存在时滞，基建对传统建筑链的拉动或更多在四季度后半段逐
步显现。另一方面，服务消费仍是扩大内需更具确定性的方向。9 月多部
门出台金融支持服务业扩能提质政策，叠加扩大消费“十五五”规划对养

### Page 4

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 4 
老、托育、文旅、健康等领域的部署，服务消费有望继续快于商品消费。

风险提示：外需边际走弱、地缘政治超预期恶化、AI 产业链降温超预期、
政策资金投放节奏不及预期。

1. GDP：实际增长边际回升 
实际 GDP 边际回升， 名义增长继续快于实际增长。三季度经济增长呈现三个特征，
一是实际增长较二季度有改善。 三季度实际GDP 同比增长 4.5%， 高于二季度的4.3%，
但仍略低于上半年平均水平；前三季度 GDP 累计增长 4.6%，较上半年回落 0.1 个百分
点。分产业看，实际 GDP 回升主要来自第二产业改善。第二产业增加值增速由二季度
的 3.0%回升至 3.4%， 第三产业由5.1%小幅升至 5.2%， 第一产业则由3.7%回落至 3.6%。
二是名义 GDP 继续快于实际 GDP，第二产业价格修复仍是重要支撑。 根据现价计算，
三季度名义 GDP 同比增长约 6.0%，较二季度提高 0.1 个百分点，高于实际 GDP 增速
1.5 个百分点。按名义与实际增速差近似估算，三季度 GDP 平减指数约为 1.5%，较二
季度的 1.6%略有回落， 指向整体价格修复仍在延续， 但没有进一步加快。 结构上， 第二
产业价格为主要支撑。二季度，第一产业平减指数由-3.3%回升至-1.7%，第三产业则由
1.3%回落至 0.7%， 第二产业平减指数约3.0%升至 3.6%， 其中工业由3.8%上升至 4.4%，
其表现与国际能源价格上涨以及 AI 产业链、 有色和电子设备价格较强基本一致。三是内
需端对经济增长的支撑边际回落，其中投资为最大拖累，外需端支撑边际增强。 支出法
看， 前三季度最终消费支出对经济增长的贡献率为45.7%， 较上半年回落0.3 个百分点；
资本形成总额贡献率为 28.3%，较上半年回落 7.8 个百分点；货物和服务净出口贡献率
为 26.1%，较上半年提升 8.2 个百分点。

请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 8 
2. 生产：工业继续回升 
工业生产继续回升，采矿业和电热燃水业改善显著。9 月，规上工业增加值同比增
长 5.5%，较 8 月提高 0.3 个百分点，环比增长 0.6%。总量上看，出口对工业生产的拉
动延续。9 月，出口交货值同比增长 17.0%，较 8 月提高 5.9 个百分点。结构上看，工
业生产回升有两方面原因。一方面，制造业增加值维持韧性，高增长仍集中在装备制造
和高技术制造领域。9 月，制造业增加值增长 6.0%，较 8 月小幅回落 0.1 个百分点，但
仍高于整体工业增速。其中，高技术制造业增长 15.3%；金属制品、通用设备、专用设
备、 铁路船舶航空航天、 电气机械和计算机通信电子设备制造业分别增长 8.2%、8.5%、
12.1%、11.7%、8.1%和 16.4%。下游消费制造业总体保持正增。农副食品加工、食品
制造、 酒饮料和精制茶、 纺织、 医药和汽车制造业分别增长4.1%、7.3%、5.5%、3.2%、
4.9%和 6.7%。另一方面，拖累项增加值边际修复。一是采矿业由负转正，对工业生产
的拖累明显减轻。9 月，采矿业增加值由下降 1.4%转为增长 1.3%，其中煤炭开采和洗
选业降幅由 5.6%收窄至 3.3%，石油和天然气开采业增速由 6.2%升至 9.8%，我们理解
部分原因是地缘局势提高能源安全和保供要求，国内油气 增储上产持续推进，为采矿业
生产提供了支撑。二是电热燃水业增速回升。电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增加
值增长 5.6%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点。 从实物产量看， 核电、 风电和太阳能发电分别
增长 21.5%、10.6%和 19.5%。 
服务业生产明显改善，生产性服务业延续支撑，生活性服务有修复。 9 月，服务业
生产指数同比增长 4.8%，较8 月提高 0.7 个百分点； 一是数字信息和生产配套型服务业
继续保持较快增长。信息传输软件和信息技术服务业、租赁和商务服务业、交通运输仓
储和邮政业生产指数分别增长 9.8%、9.5%和 5.4%。二是服务业改善广度有提升。 住宿
和餐饮业生产指数增长 5.3%，服务业商务活动指数由 49.3%升至 50.2%，重新进入扩
张区间； 电信广播电视及卫星传输、 货币金融和保险等行业商务活动指数均位于55%以
上。 
图5 采矿业规上增加值显著回升（%）

图6 规上工业增加值环比高于季节性（%）

3. 投资：延续回落 
投资低位企稳。9 月， 固定资产投资累计同比下降7.2%， 降幅与 8 月持平。按累计
金额反推，9 月当月投资同比约下降 7.2%，较 8 月的下降 11.0%明显收窄； 剔除房地产
后， 当月投资同比约下降3.4%， 降幅较8 月收窄约 4.4 个百分点。 季调环比增长0.07%，
是 3 月以来首次转正。 
图8 固定资产投资低位企稳（%）

### Page 10

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 10 
制造业投资小幅修复， 增长仍集中在少数优势领域。9 月， 制造业累计投资下降2.1%，
降幅较 1—8 月收窄 0.2 个百分点。 结构上看， 一是正增行业仍主要集中在装备和新兴制
造领域。国家统计局公布的 13 个制造业行业中，只有 5 个保持正增长，其中铁路船舶
航空航天、 计算机通信电子设备制造业投资分别增长15.8%和 9.6%， 纺织、 电气机械和
通用设备制造业分别增长 4.2%、1.2%和 1.1%。高技术产业投资增长 4.9%，其中信息
服务、 航空航天器及设备、 电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长20.2%、12.5%和 7.6%。
二是上游原材料和下游消费制造业投资仍较弱。 农副食品加工、 食品制造、 化工、 医药、
有色金属冶炼、 金属制品、 专用设备和汽车制造业投资分别下降2.5%、10.3%、7.5%、
8.1%、5.1%、10.1%、8.9%和 6.3%。

4. 消费：改善相对明显，服务强于商品 
消费是内需端改善最明显的分项， 商品消费仍受汽车拖累。9 月， 社零同比增长1.1%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.35%，剔除汽车后社零同比增长 3.9%，较 8 月
加快 1.4 个百分点。 结构上看， 商品和餐饮消费同步恢复。9 月， 商品零售额增长1.0%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点； 餐饮收入增长2.1%， 提高1.0 个百分点。 从更广义的消费口
径看， 服务消费仍是消费的重要支撑。 三季度， 社会消费商品和服务零售总额增长2.6%，
其中服务零售额增长 5.0%， 明显高于商品零售额的 1.0%； 网上服务零售额增长5.3%。
通讯信息、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务零售额保持较快增长。商品消费方面，必选消
费和通讯器材维持韧性，汽车是主要拖累。服装、化妆品、日用品、家电和中西药品零
售额分别增长 1.2%、5.4%、4.1%、1.9%和 5.0%，通讯器材类增长 31.1%。汽车零售
额下降 21.2%， 降幅较8 月扩大 2.7 个百分点； 家具、 建筑装潢材料仍分别下降3.0%和
9.2%，降幅较 8 月有所收窄。 
图15 9 月社零增速边际修复（%）

图16 餐饮、商品消费均回升（%）

5. 展望：有望延续生产较强、内需修复格局 
展望后续，四季度经济有望延续生产较强、需求渐进修复的格局，未来增量支撑主
要来自两条线索。总体看，三季度实际增长边际企稳，优势制造业、外需和服务消费构
成主要支撑。 往后看， 增量支撑线索有两方面。 一方面， 以 “六张网” 和8000 亿元新型
政策性金融工具为抓手，加快资金投放和项目开工。9 月以来三家政策性银行投放的新
型政策性金融工具资金已经超过 1000 亿元，建筑业 PMI 和新订单指数同步回升，指向
政策正在向项目端传导。不过，从补充资本金、落实配套融资到形成施工和实物工作量
仍存在时滞，基建对传统建筑链的拉动或更多在四季度后半段逐步显现。另一方面，服
务消费仍是扩大内需更具确定性的方向。9 月多部门出台金融支持服务业扩能提质政策，
叠加扩大消费“十五五”规划对养老、托育、文旅、健康等领域的部署，服务消费有望
继续快于商品消费。 
风险提示 
外需边际走弱、地缘政治超预期恶化、AI 产业链降温超预期、政策资金投放节奏不
及预期。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 19 - 23

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 19 - 23
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week’s inflation reports came in hot. September’s CPI rose 0.6%
m/m, lifting headline inflation from 3.4% y/y in August to 3.7%. Core CPI
rose 0.3% m/m, taking its y/y rate from 2.4% to 2.6%. Both topped
expectations. PPI final demand jumped 1.3% m/m. That pushed its y/y
rate to 6.3%, the highest since 2022. Retail sales rose 0.8% m/m, though
just 0.2% excluding gas stations.
Odds of a 25bps hike at the October 27-28 FOMC meeting fell from 22%

before the CPI report to 17%, despite the hot inflation data. Fed Chair
Kevin Warsh offered no signal on October's meeting at the IMF and World
Bank annual meetings in Bangkok on Friday. “I don’t prejudge meetings,”
he said. Odds of a December hike rose. We still expect the Fed to reverse
the remaining two of last year’s three 25bps “insurance” cuts. Fed officials
are now in their pre-meeting blackout period.
This week’s data calendar is light, with earnings season taking center

stage. Friday’s flash PMIs are the main US releases. The Fed’s Financial
Stability Report and Canada’s federal budget are tentatively due Tuesday.
Here’s more:
(1) ADP & Unemployment Claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading
(Tue) follows a rise in the four-week average to 24,750 for the week ended
September 26, the highest since early June and up from a late-July
bottom of 8,250 (chart).
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 199,000, with the four-week

average at 198,500 (chart). Continuing claims fell to 1,710,000 for the

### Page 4

week ended October 2, matching their four-week average. The labor
market continues to improve.
(2) Flash PMIs. S&P Global’s flash M-PMI and NM-PMI for October (Fri)
follow September readings of 55.9 and 58.8 (chart). The services prices
component is worth watching after ISM’s NM-PMI prices-paid index
jumped to 70.7 in September, the highest since July 2022.
Flash PMIs for Australia and Japan (Thu) and for the Eurozone, Germany,
France, and the UK (Fri) also land this week. September’s M-PMIs were

52.9 for the Eurozone and 52.1 for the UK.
(3) Earnings. The earnings reporting season picks up this week. Netflix,
GE Aerospace, and Northrop Grumman report on Tuesday. Tesla and
Meta, the first of the Magnificent-7 to report, follow on Wednesday along

### Page 5

with Lam Research, IBM, and Texas Instruments. Spotify, Bloom Energy,
Western Digital, Lockheed Martin, and Blackstone report on Thursday.
ASML and TSMC posted record Q3 results last week. ASML’s net sales
rose to €11.7 billion from €9.3 billion in Q2, with the gross margin up to
57.5% from 54.0%. TSMC’s revenue jumped 50.9% y/y. Analysts’
consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for S&P 500
companies of 24.0% y/y (chart).
(4) Global. China’s Q3 GDP (Mon) follows Q2’s 4.3% y/y growth (chart).

We expect a moderate pickup in activity. China’s unemployment rate for
September is also due, following August’s 5.2% rate.
UK CPI (Wed) for September follows August’s 3.1% y/y headline and
2.6% core (chart). Rising energy and input costs point to a further pickup,

### Page 6

with the input PPI up 8.2% y/y in Q2. Bank of England Governor Andrew
Bailey speaks later that day. UK labor market data (Tue) and PPI (Wed)
are also due.
Canada’s CPI (Mon) for September follows August’s 3.0% y/y headline
and 2.3% core (chart).

Document 299: Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm một lần tăng nữa trong năm (2026-09-17)

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

Fed tăng lãi suất, dự báo thêm 
một lần tăng nữa trong năm 
Đánh giá áp lực lên tỷ giá và lãi suất Việt Nam

Fed tăng lãi suất và dự báo có thêm một lần tăng lãi suất nữa trong năm 
— Trong kỳ họp FOMC ngày 15–16/09/2026, Fed quyết định tăng 25bps lãi suất điều hành 
lên mức 3.75%–4%, đánh dấu lần tăng lãi suất đầu tiên kể từ năm 2023. Quyết định 
được thông qua với tỷ lệ đồng thuận 12–0, phản ánh sự thống nhất của các thành viên 
FOMC trong việc ưu tiên kiểm soát lạm phát. 
— Chủ tịch Fed Kevin Warsh nhấn mạnh lạm phát vẫn ở mức cao và cần được đưa về mục 
tiêu 2% trong thời gian phù hợp hơn. Bên cạnh đó, Fed đánh giá hoạt động kinh tế 
đang tăng trưởng vững, tiêu dùng nội địa duy trì tích cực, năng suất và đầu tư vốn tăng 
trưởng mạnh, trong khi thị trường lao động vẫn ổn định. Điều này cho thấy Fed chưa 
nhận thấy áp lực suy giảm kinh tế đủ lớn để ưu tiên nới lỏng chính sách tiền tệ.  Ông 
Warsh không đưa ra cam kết cụ thể về thời điểm tăng lãi suất, nhấn mạnh chính sách 
sẽ phụ thuộc vào diễn biến lạm phát, thị trường lao động và các dữ liệu kinh tế sắp tới. 
— Biểu đồ dot plot tháng 9 cho thấy Fed vẫn nghiêng về khả năng tăng thêm lãi suất 
trong năm 2026, với 16/18 thành viên dự báo ít nhất một lần tăng nữa, đưa lãi suất điều 
hành lên vùng 4%–4.25%. Tuy nhiên, sự phân hóa trong dự báo lãi suất năm 2027 
(3.625%–4.375%) cho thấy đây có thể là một lần điều chỉnh nhằm kiểm soát lạm phát, 
thay vì tín hiệu về một chu kỳ thắt chặt kéo dài.

Quan điểm của KBSV đối với lãi suất và tỷ giá 
— Trong 3 tháng cuối năm, chúng tôi đánh giá nhiều khả năng Fed sẽ có thêm một lần 
tăng lãi suất 25bps, dựa trên:  
(1) Fed tiếp tục nâng dự báo PCE headline và core 2026 lên thêm 10 bps so với dự 
báo hồi tháng 6, đồng thời dự báo lạm phát sẽ quay về mức mục tiêu 2% vào năm 
2029 (thay vì năm 2028 như dự báo trước đó), trong khi dự báo về tỷ lệ thất 
nghiệp và tăng trưởng GDP được điều chỉnh theo hướng tích cực hơn. 
(2) Giá dầu vẫn duy trì ở mức cao (dự kiến quay về vùng 9x USD/thùng vào quý 4) 
trong bối cảnh xung đột tại Trung Đông tiếp tục gây gián đoạn hoạt động vận 
chuyển và xuất khẩu dầu mỏ. Mặc dù Saudi Arabia đã tăng cường vận chuyển 
dầu qua Oman, qua đó giảm bớt lo ngại về thiếu hụt nguồn cung trong ngắn hạn, 
rủi ro gián đoạn vẫn hiện hữu khi xung đột chưa được giải quyết hoàn toàn. 
— Chúng tôi nâng dự báo DXY lên vùng 98–103 điểm trong phần còn lại của năm với động 
lực hỗ trợ USD trong ngắn hạn đến từ việc Fed phát tín hiệu tiếp tục thắt chặt, trong khi 
thị trường điều chỉnh kỳ vọng lãi suất theo hướng duy trì cao hơn. Tuy nhiên, việc các 
NHTW lớn khác cũng duy trì định hướng kiểm soát lạm phát có thể hạn chế mức độ mở 
rộng chênh lệch lãi suất, qua đó làm giảm khả năng USD tăng giá một chiều và DXY 
tăng mạnh khỏi vùng dự báo.

### Page 2

— Chúng tôi dự báo vĩ mô trong nước đối với: (1) tỷ giá USD/VND tới cuối năm 2026 đi 
ngang so với cuối 2025 nhờ nguồn cung USD tích cực (từ kiều hối, FDI giải ngân và nâng 
hạng thị trường vốn) và chênh lệch lãi suất VND-USD dù có xu hướng thu hẹp nhưng 
vẫn duy trì trạng thái dương; và (2) lãi suất huy động sẽ duy trì ở vùng hiện tại trong 
phần còn lại của năm và kỳ vọng có thể giảm nhẹ nếu lạm phát được kiểm soát và 
NHNN sẽ có biện pháp hỗ trợ thanh khoản thị trường tích cực hơn.

Document 317: How AI agents could reshape retail as stores move beyond sales

Source PDF: all report pdf/How AI agents could reshape retail as stores move beyond sales.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

Agentic commerce, where autonomous AI agents
independently select and purchase products on behalf of
consumers, is forcing retailers to rethink their role. As AI takes
over more of the buying process, physical stores are evolving
from a sales channel into a destination for advice, service,
experience, and omnichannel support
Katinka Jongkind
A Galaxy General AI robot retail store, Shanghai. Retail is entering a new phase
of digital disruption, where agentic commerce could fundamentally change

how consumers shop
Agentic commerce fundamentally changes
the buying process
Retail is entering a new phase of digital disruption. E-commerce
has already transformed where consumers shop; agentic
commerce is set to change how they shop. Autonomous artiﬁcial
intelligence agents can search, compare and purchase products on
behalf of consumers, shifting shopping from an active task to an
increasingly automated background process.

### Page 2

Agentic commerce goes beyond AI
assistants and comparison websites
Key diﬀerences between agentic commerce, AI assistants (such as
ChatGPT and Gemini) and comparison websites
Source: ING Research
Most relevant for routine purchases, less so
for luxury goods
Agentic commerce is not equally suited to every product category.
Its greatest potential lies in repeat and replacement purchases, as
well as functional, standardised products. In these categories, AI

agents can eﬃciently compare prices, speciﬁcations, and
availability, while using consumption patterns to determine the
optimal time to buy.
Adoption is likely to be slower for luxury, trend-sensitive and
experience-driven products. In these categories, inspiration,
emotion, and personal preference remain central to the purchasing
decision, making consumers less inclined to relinquish control. AI
agents can assist by pre-selecting products that match predeﬁned

preferences, but ﬁnal purchasing decisions are likely to remain
ﬁrmly in the hands of the consumer.

### Page 3

Consumer electronics and personal care
lead the adoption of agentic commerce
Within non-food retail, agentic commerce appears particularly well
suited to consumer electronics and personal care. Both sectors are
characterised by a high degree of product standardisation and a
large share of repeat purchases, making them ideal candidates for
AI-driven purchasing.
In consumer electronics, purchasing decisions are often based on
objective and easily comparable criteria, including price, technical

speciﬁcations, and availability. This makes products such as
televisions, laptops and other electronic devices well suited to
automation, allowing AI agents to manage much of the purchasing
journey from product selection to the ﬁnal transaction.
The potential is equally signiﬁcant in personal care, where many
purchases are routine and repetitive. Products such as diapers,
shampoo, and toothpaste can be reordered automatically based
on usage patterns and preset preferences. By reducing the eﬀort

required to order everyday products, AI agents could further
accelerate the shift towards online shopping.
Agentic commerce is best suited to
consumer electronics and personal care
products
Product categories most and least suited to agentic commerce

### Page 4

Source: ING Research
Personal taste limits AI agents' potential in
fashion and living
For now, fashion and home furnishings appear less suited to fully
autonomous agentic commerce. Purchasing decisions in these
categories are often shaped by inspiration, aesthetics, personal
taste, and broader lifestyle considerations, making consumers less
willing to hand over control to an AI agent.
While AI can support product discovery and narrow down options

based on predeﬁned preferences, the ﬁnal purchase decision will
typically remain with the consumer. In these segments, AI is
therefore more likely to serve as a decision-support tool than as a
fully autonomous purchasing agent.
Chinese platforms lead adoption
Agentic commerce creates the greatest opportunities for large
global marketplaces and technology companies. These players
combine vast product assortments with the scale, data and
infrastructure needed to deploy AI agents eﬀectively. Their

integrated ecosystems, including payment solutions, logistics
networks, and data platforms, enable them to integrate AI agents
into their business operations faster and more eﬀectively.
Chinese platforms such as Alibaba and JD.com currently lead the
way, with AI agents already deeply integrated into their respective
platform networks. These agents can not only provide product

### Page 5

recommendations but also complete purchases autonomously.
Meanwhile, US technology companies including Amazon, Google,
Meta, OpenAI and Anthropic are investing heavily in the
development and rollout of AI agents, intensifying the global race
for leadership in this emerging market.
European players are structurally lagging behind their American
and Chinese competitors. Stricter regulation and less integrated
platform networks limit their ability to bundle scale, data and

services. While Zalando is among Europe’s frontrunners in AI-
powered retail, its applications are currently focused on product
discovery, comparison, and selection. Fully autonomous
purchasing has yet to become a reality.
Technology platforms are becoming the
gatekeepers of commerce
AI agents are rarely neutral actors. Embedded within commercial
ecosystems, they are inherently linked to the interests of platforms,
marketplaces and technology companies. Although agentic

commerce promises greater convenience and time savings for
consumers, it is also likely to strengthen the dominance of large
tech ﬁrms and marketplaces. These parties are becoming
increasingly inﬂuential in shaping product choices and controlling
consumer access.
Physical stores are not disappearing, but
their role is changing
How will the role of the physical store evolve in an AI-driven retail
landscape?

### Page 6

Source: ING Research
Redeﬁning the store in the age of AI
Agentic commerce is expected to accelerate the shift of sales to
online channels, putting further pressure on the existence of
physical stores. The extent of this impact will largely depend on
consumers’ willingness to delegate purchasing decisions to AI
agents.
Physical stores will nevertheless remain relevant for many non-food
retailers. Their role is shifting from sales channel to destination for

advice, service, inspiration, and support of online sales. Stores that
continue to rely mainly on transactional sales risk losing relevance
in an increasingly AI-driven retail landscape.
Retailers that successfully integrate their physical and digital
channels, deﬁne clear roles for their stores, and deploy technology
strategically can strengthen their position within the customer
journey. In a future shaped by AI-driven commerce, their role will

increasingly centre on adding value where automation cannot.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research 
– 
2

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.4% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4,25% - 4,50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth slowed to 4.3% y/y in Q2 2026 vs. 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth 
remains characterized by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports and the fragility of sectors that rely 
on domestic demand  has even widened this year . Domestic demand has weakened significantly in recent months . Retail sales have barely 
increased, the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting 
economic growth are expected to increase in the second half of the year. CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.0% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 2.8% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

Contacts 
Stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com  
Tarik.rharrab@bnpparibas.com

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.4% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would ease slightly to 1.2% in 2026 (1.3% in 2025), before edging back to 1.3% in 2027. The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averaging 3.2% in both 2026 and 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting second-
round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. The BoE 
is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
• The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index* has edged up slightly since late
August, rising 0.2pts to 159.1.
• Quarterly growth momentum has
seen a further lift and is now tracking
towards a 1.7% gain for the September
quarter which would mark the strongest

quarterly rise since June 2025.
• However, both the monthly and weekly
reads show momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter. After posting
0.7-0.9%mth gains through May, June
and July, the Index rose just 0.2%
in August and is tracking a similarly
subdued 0.3% pace for September.
Weekly reads are also tracking a more
moderate trend pace of 0.2%mth.
• On a quarterly basis, the detail shows
discretionary services and international
transactions have led the pick-up.

While some of this looks to be catch-
up from travel disruptions in the June
quarter, gains have been consistent and
broadly based. Likewise, there is little
evidence that the pick-up is coming from
a lift in prices with recent CPI updates
suggesting services inflation has been
steady rather than accelerating (see
here).
• On the apparent loss of momentum
over the course of the quarter, the detail
suggests this is a re-run of the fuel price
squeeze in March-April-May with the

essentials spend gain rising strongly. So
far the spillover effects are showing up
mainly as a dampening in discretionary
goods spend with the slowing in
discretionary services largely confined to
hospitality.
• Overall, the card tracker data suggests
the Q3 spending data will still come in
as a relatively solid gain, even allowing
for a firmer gain in prices in the quarter.
However, the fade in momentum is
notable with the impact of further fuel

pricer increases and this week’s RBA rate
rise still to come. Add what now looks
more likely to be a follow-on interest
rate hike in November and the December
quarter is shaping as a significant
slowdown in card activity.
“... both the monthly
and weekly reads show
momentum has faded over
the course of the quarter.”

3
• Chart 2 shows how quarterly growth
in the Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index compares to growth in nominal
consumption as reported in the quarterly
national accounts. All estimates are
adjusted for regular seasonal variations.
Quarterly growth momentum is now

tracking around 1.7%, the strongest pace
since the fuel price spike in March, and
pior to that, since the turn of the year.
• Notably, growth ex fuel has slowed
since the start of September, tracking a
slower 1.4% quarterly pace in the latest
week. This reflects both the end of the
temporary halving in fuel excise tax and
the latest resurgence in energy prices
globally.
• Recall that Q2 national accounts showed
nominal spending (excluding housing

costs, which are not covered in our card
tracker indicators) rose 0.9%qtr, similar
to Q1’s 1%qtr gain but with annual
growth slowing to 4.7%yr from 5.5%yr.
Growth in real, inflation-adjusted terms
was more subdued at +0.4%qtr, 1.8%yr
(near flat in per capita terms). The detail
was ‘messy’ with significant disruptions
from the Middle East conflict (see here).
• The card tracker data now has 12 of the
13 weeks of Q3. As such, the 1.7%qtr
rise in nominal terms and price rise in the

1%qtr range points to a real, inflation
adjusted gain of around 0.7%qtr, in line
with Westpac’s current forecasts.

• Chart 4 shows quarterly growth in card
activity across broad categories. The year
has been marked by rotations between
essentials and discretionary categories
as fuel price shocks have washed in
and out. The switch from essentials to
discretionary categories – services in

particular – in the early part of Q3 is again
reversing with the main drag coming in
discretionary goods.
• In quarterly growth terms, essential
goods is now leading, running at 2.5%qtr,
with discretionary services still relatively
strong at 2.2%qtr, essential services
tracking more slowly at 1.4%qtr and
discretionary goods much weaker at just
0.3%qtr (flat to down in per capita terms).
• Digging into the more detailed
discretionary services segments, the

initial quarterly pickup came from a big
turnaround in transport services, which
look to be partly a rebound from air travel
disruptions in Q2 with a similar pattern
evident in ‘international transactions’
(which are not included in discretionary
services category, as they include some
goods purchases, but are presented here
as corroborating evidence). The charts
on p6-7 provide some more detail on the
other categories. Notably the weakening
in discretionary goods momentum looks

to be broad-based.
• Regionally, the softening is a little more
evident in Vic than in other states.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 9
About the Westpac card data indicators
The indicators presented in this report are based on the millions of credit and debit card
transactions processed every day. Transactions covering over ten million merchants are
classified into over 700 categories. These are in turn grouped into higher level aggregates

that provide a timely guide to wider economic trends.
The main focus of these indicators is consumer spending. Where possible, we have sought
to exclude ‘non spending’ transactions such as: money transfers; tax payments; loan
repayments; charitable donations; and superannuation contributions.
It should also be noted that these indicators will also be affected by shifts between card and
non-card transactions. This was a significant factor during the COVID–19 pandemic – health

concerns about the use of physical cash leading to significantly higher use of cards vs cash,
particularly where contact–less transactions are available. Transaction flows also include
reversals/refunds which were also a significant phenomenon during the onset of COVID,
especially in areas such as travel.
All transaction data is compiled at a highly aggregated level so that individual customer or
merchant data is never revealed.
Index construction

The key metrics used in this report are indexes of spending–related card activity where the
base of 100 is average activity in 2019. As an example, if transaction flows are 5% above their
average level in 2019, the index read for the period is 105. If flows in a subsequent period are
8% above the average level in 2019, the index read for this period is 108. Growth between the
two periods can be calculated simply as the change between the two index reads, i.e. 2.9%.

All measures are adjusted for regular seasonality. Weekly estimates are generated using
the US Bureau of Labor’s MoveReg weekly seasonal adjustment program. Note that in some
cases, high levels of volatility during the COVID period mean it is not possible to produce
seasonally adjusted estimates for some historical periods.
Also, note that previous versions of this report used different approaches to seasonal
adjustment and measurement more generally. This means Index reads are sometimes not

directly comparable. See the ‘About the Westpac card data indicators’ sections from earlier
reports to more detail.
Classifications
Note that the measures used for card data and in this report do not align completely with
the those used in official ABS statistics, including the ABS household spending indicator,
ABS retail trade survey and ABS estimate of consumer spending published in the national
accounts. There are a range of differences including around both coverage and classification.

As such, the card data should be treated as broadly indicative.
The transaction data is grouped into 26 categories that are then combined into four main as
follows:
Discretionary goods: alcoholic beverages, tobacco, clothing & footwear, furnishings &
household equipment, household appliances, vehicle–related, recreational & cultural goods,
newspaper, books & stationery, and other personal effects.
Discretionary services: transport services (part), recreational & cultural services, gambling,

catering services, accommodation services, other personal care, insurance & financial
services, other services.
Essential goods: food & non alcoholic beverages, medical products, appliances & equipment,
and operation of personal transport equipment.
Essential services: electricity, gas & other fuels, health services, transport services (part),
communications and education.
The report also uses two additional classifications:
MHSI/Retail: based on the extent to which categories cover activity that is in scope for the

ABS monthly household spending indicator and ABS retail trade survey.

Document 414: “换锚”不等于“降息”

Source PDF: all report pdf/“换锚”不等于“降息”.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

⼴发宏观钟林楠认为，创设⾮银流动性⽀持⼯具直达公募基⾦和理财、政策利率锚定隔夜
品种、贷款定价基准⾛向多元、正式退出存贷款基准利率。这绝⾮简单 “ 类降息 ” ，⽽是货币
政策框架从数量型向价格型转型的关键⼀跃。债券市场勿形成单边预期，权益资产则应告
别单线索、拓宽配置⼴谱性。
摘要：
摘要
10 ⽉ 19 ⽇，央⾏在⾦融街论坛上推出若⼲项政策，其中有四项值得重点关注：（ 1 ）会同⾦融监
管总局、中国证监会设⽴应对⾮银机构⼤额赎回的流动性⽀持⼯具；（ 2 ）研究择机把公开市场
主要操作品种的期限切换为隔夜，公开市场隔夜回购利率将相应成为主要政策利率；（ 3 ）推动
贷款利率定价基准改⾰，从⽐较单⼀地参考贷款市场报价利率（ LPR ）定价向更多元的定价基准
转型；（ 4 ）退出存贷款基准利率。对这四项举措，我们有以下理解。
第⼀，⾸先是创设⾮银流动性⽀持⼯具，我们可以从流动性⻛险管理和宏观审慎框架优化的⻆
度去理解。官⽅的公开表述最早出现在去年 10 ⽉⾦融街论坛；今年 1 ⽉央⾏⼯作会议再次指出

“ 建⽴在特定情景下向⾮银机构提供流动性的机制性安排 ” ，且将其置于 “ 化解重点领域⾦融⻛险 ”
部分，显⽰这⼀⼯具设⽴初衷是防⻛险维护⾦融稳定。 6 ⽉央⾏在陆家嘴论坛上将措辞进⼀步升
级为 “ 研究设⽴特定情景⾮银流动性⽀持宏观审慎⼯具 ” ，将其进⼀步定义为宏观审慎⼯具⽽⾮货
币政策⽇常⼯具。⾦融街论坛上关于 “ 设⽴应对⾮银机构⼤额赎回的流动性⽀持⼯具 ” 的界定，⼀
是从 “ 研究设⽴ ” 到 “ 设⽴ ” ，显⽰政策进⼊落地阶段；⼆是进⼀步将 “ 特定情景 ” 具体为 “ ⾮银机构⼤
额赎回 ” ，明确了使⽤条件。
第⼆，⾮银流动性⽀持⼯具的要素和操作细节仍有待明确，⽬前有两点相对确定：（ 1 ）主要针
对债券市场。潘功胜⾏⻓指出 “ ⽬的是建⽴债券市场稳定机制，在资管机构⾯临群体性流动性⻛
险、债券市场出现赎回 — 抛售负向循环的特定情景下启⽤ ” 。近些年流动性⻛险和负反馈的典型
案例是 2022 年四季度，政策变化带来⻛偏和基本⾯预期上修，债券市场出现了 “ 利率快速上⾏ —

理财净值回撤 — 客⼾赎回 — 抛售债券 — 利率加速上⾏ — 理财净值进⼀步回撤 ” 的循环。在类似情
景下，央⾏只能通过加⼤对银⾏体系的流动性⽀持，借助 “ 银⾏ — ⾮银 ” 的流动性传递路径来稳定
⾮银负债进⽽稳定市场，政策效果会打折扣，在部分情景下（⽐如信⽤⻛险引发的负反馈）还
可能出现造成流动性淤积过于宽松，滋⽣资⾦空转套利；⽽设⽴新⼯具后，流动性可直达⾮银
机构，增强政策效⼒同时也避免流动性⼤幅波动；（ 2 ）操作可能是以 “ 互换 ” 形式，主要⾯向公募
基⾦、理财及其对应的证券⺟公司与银⾏⺟公司。依据《中国⼈⺠银⾏法》，央⾏不能直接对⾮
银机构发放贷款。今年 6 ⽉央⾏在陆家嘴论坛上提出研究 “ 机构群体性⾯临流动性危机且可能引发
系统性⻛险时，通过互换⽅式向⾮银机构提供紧急流动性 ” 。
第三，再来看公开市场操作主要品种切换为隔夜品种、政策利率切换为隔夜利率。7 ⽉中旬央⾏
曾提出逐步增加隔夜逆回购操作频次，之后隔夜逆回购操作频次和规模明显升⾼，市场对于进
⼀步改⾰有所预期。 10 ⽉以来 10 年和 30 年国债利率有不同程度的下⾏，其中可能包含对这⼀改

⾰趋势的定价。我们理解，这⼀举措主要⽬的是提⾼市场利率和政策利率期限的匹配度，完善
短端利率调控机制。今年年初以来，央⾏已多次向外传递市场基准利率应该更加关注 DR001 这
种隔夜品种，其成交量占⽐常年在 90% 以上，更能反映货币市场的真实情况，对于政策⽽⾔更
短期的市场基准利率调控难度会更低，海外⼤部分成熟央⾏都锚定隔夜品种；⽽央⾏主要操作
品种为 7 天逆回购， DR001 对标的政策利率为 7 天逆回购利率，在期限上明显错配。将公开市场
操作品种切换为隔夜、政策利率切换为隔夜逆回购利率，⼀则有助于提⾼流动性管理的精细
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

度，更好的 “ 削峰填⾕ ” ，提⾼政策对市场利率的掌控⼒；⼆则实现市场利率与政策利率期限匹
配，理顺政策利率传导机制。
第四，关于贷款基准利率从单⼀转为多元基准，央⾏在今年⼀季度货币政策专栏中有过国际经
验的相关介绍；今年三季度部分银⾏已有相关实践，发放了利率挂钩 DR001 或 DR007 的贷款。
在前期报告《 26Q1 货政报告的六点增量信息》中，我们对其有过详细解读：主要经济体贷款利
率定价基准多元，⼀个重要原因在于直接融资的发展让企业部⻔能够以更低的成本、更加市场
化的利率来融资，进⽽倒逼贷款利率市场化。海外不少国家案例显⽰，融资渠道多元的⼤型企
业的贷款定价基准均是市场利率，⽽较难发债融资的中⼩型企业与零售客⼾贷款定价基准仍挂
钩政策利率。⽬前我国企业债券融资利率与贷款利率的利差已经明显拉开，直接融资对信贷的
替代作⽤愈发明显；为适应债券利率的下⾏，越来越多的贷款以 LPR 减点的利率来发放，今年上
半年 LPR 减点发放的贷款⽐重接近 50% ，明显⾼于 2019 年 LPR 改⾰时的⽔平。为适应直接融资

的发展、保持 LPR 的引导作⽤、促进社会综合融资成本低位运⾏，我国贷款有必要逐步转向多元
基准利率定价。在 LPR 之外，银⾏若再有意愿选择某⼀市场利率作为补充性贷款基准利率，理论
上⾄少需满⾜两个条件：⼀是能充分反映银⾏负债成本，⼆是波动偏低。由此推断，未来政策
稳定货币市场利率、降低波动的必要性会更为凸显。从 6 ⽉陆家嘴论坛⾄此次⾦融街论坛，央⾏
创设隔夜逆回购品种、将隔夜逆回购作为主要操作品种、政策利率切换为隔夜利率、缩窄隐性
利率⾛廊（临时隔夜正逆回购利率区间）等完善短端利率调控举措，与贷款基准利率多元化相
辅相成。
第五，退出存贷款基准利率更多是完成制度上的收尾、补全利率市场化框架的完整性，如央⾏
所指出的 “ ⾦融机构存贷款基准利率⾃ 2015 年 10 ⽉以来，⻓期未做调整，在存贷款定价中实际上
已不再发挥作⽤，⽬前只剩下在少数司法领域作为计算利息的依据 ” 。在⾦融领域，贷款定价⼤
致经历了三个阶段：（ 1 ） 2013 年之前盯住贷款基准利率；（ 2 ） 2013 年放开贷款利率管制，新定

价基准未形成，处于换锚的过渡期，实际定价中部分银⾏的贷款利率依然会参考贷款基准利
率；（ 3 ） 2019 年 LPR 改⾰之后，贷款利率定价锚定 LPR 。后续按照央⾏政策演进脉络，贷款基
准可能进⼀步变为 “LPR+ 市场利率（如 DR ） ” 。存款利率定价变化更加缓慢。央⾏在 2015 年名义
上放开存款利率管制，存款利率定价实际受⾃律机制牵引， 2022 年之后由于贷款利率单边跟随
LPR 和政策利率调整明显下⾏，存款利率定价调整缓慢，息差压⼒升⾼，央⾏宣布建⽴存款利率
调整机制，存款利率跟随 LPR 和 10 年国债利率调整，⽽ LPR 实际受政策利率（ 7 天逆回购利率）
牵引，所以在 2022 年之后，对于⾦融市场⽽⾔存贷款利率定价实际都统归于政策利率，利率传
导机制为 “ 政策利率 —LPR/10 年国债到期收益率 / 货币市场短端利率 — 存贷款利率与其他债券利
率 ” 。
第六，综上所述，我们不能简单地从 “ 类降息 ” 等狭义视⻆去理解政策举措，四项举措⼀⽅⾯有
助于维护流动性合理充裕、畅通利率传导；另⼀⽅⾯，它是既定的也是货币政策框架由数量型

调控向价格型调控转型、利率市场化改⾰的延续。关于政策利率与⼴谱利率以及实体融资成本
的后续传导机制和主要特征，仍具有⼀定复杂性，需要进⼀步观察。
第七，最后再看对资产定价的影响。对于债券市场来说，政策操作品种与利率切换、贷款基准
多元化等有助于降低央⾏投放资⾦的负债成本、稳定流动性，同时⼀则前期利率曲线已有初步
定价；⼆则同⽇出来的三季度 GDP 增速略超预期，利率所⾯临的经济基本⾯预期亦会有所上
修；三则从 9 ⽉单⽉固投和消费数据反弹来看，前期⼀揽⼦政策的效果也初步显现，所以要避免
形成对利率定价的单边预期。对于权益资产⽽⾔，前期主要定价全球流动性收敛、逆全球化、 AI
产业链供需关系等，对于国内政策和基本⾯的定价相对不⾜，⾦融街论坛期间推出的⼏项举措
有利于稳定增⻓条件，顺周期和内需相关资产将得到⼀定⽀撑，所以应告别单线索、适度提升
配置⼴谱性。
正⽂

### Page 3

10 ⽉ 19 ⽇，央⾏在⾦融街论坛上推出若⼲项政策，其中有四项值得重点关注：（ 1 ）会同⾦融监
管总局、中国证监会设⽴应对⾮银机构⼤额赎回的流动性⽀持⼯具；（ 2 ）研究择机把公开市场
主要操作品种的期限切换为隔夜，公开市场隔夜回购利率将相应成为主要政策利率；（ 3 ）推动
贷款利率定价基准改⾰，从⽐较单⼀地参考贷款市场报价利率（ LPR ）定价向更多元的定价基准
转型；（ 4 ）退出存贷款基准利率。对这四项举措，我们有以下理解。
⾸先是创设⾮银流动性⽀持⼯具，我们可以从流动性⻛险管理和宏观审慎框架优化的⻆度去理
解。从央⾏官⽹来看，官⽅的公开表述最早出现在去年 10 ⽉⾦融街论坛；今年 1 ⽉央⾏⼯作会议
再次指出 “ 建⽴在特定情景下向⾮银机构提供流动性的机制性安排 ” ，且将其置于 “ 化解重点领域
⾦融⻛险 ” 部分，显⽰这⼀⼯具设⽴初衷是防⻛险维护⾦融稳定。6 ⽉央⾏在陆家嘴论坛上将措
辞进⼀步升级为 “ 研究设⽴特定情景⾮银流动性⽀持宏观审慎⼯具 ” ，将其进⼀步定义为宏观审慎

⼯具⽽⾮货币政策⽇常⼯具。⾦融街论坛上关于 “ 设⽴应对⾮银机构⼤额赎回的流动性⽀持⼯具 ”
的界定，⼀是从 “ 研究设⽴ ” 到 “ 设⽴ ” ，显⽰政策进⼊落地阶段；⼆是进⼀步将 “ 特定情景 ” 具体为
“ ⾮银机构⼤额赎回 ” ，明确了使⽤条件。
2025 年 10 ⽉，央⾏在⾦融街论坛上指出，股票市场有⽀持资本市场稳定发展的两项货币政策⼯
具，⽀持汇⾦公司发挥 “ 类平准基⾦ ” 作⽤。综合平衡维护⾦融市场稳定运⾏和防范⾦融市场道德
⻛险，探索在特定情景下向⾮银机构提供流动性的机制性安排。
2026 年 1 ⽉，央⾏召开⼯作会议，会议指出，稳妥化解重点领域⾦融⻛险。建⽴在特定情景下向
⾮银机构提供流动性的机制性安排，发挥好两项⽀持资本市场的货币政策⼯具作⽤。
2026 年 6 ⽉，央⾏在陆家嘴论坛上指出，研究设⽴特定情景⾮银流动性⽀持宏观审慎⼯具。
2024 年 9 ⽉，⼈⺠银⾏会同证监会创设两项⽀持资本市场⼯具，建⽴了中央银⾏向⾮银机构和资
本市场的流动性供给渠道，发挥了积极的作⽤。在此基础上，我们继续研究政策⼯具，当债券

等市场出现系统性压⼒，正常流动性渠道受阻，机构群体性⾯临流动性危机且可能引发系统性
⻛险时，通过互换⽅式向⾮银机构提供紧急流动性。机制设计将综合权衡维护⾦融市场稳定运
⾏和防范⾦融市场道德⻛险。 “ 特定情景 ” 意味着该机制不是对⾮银⾦融机构的常态化流动性供
给； “ 防范⾦融市场道德⻛险 ” 意味着⾮银机构与央⾏操作必须满⾜宏观审慎要求，还要提供⾼等
级抵押品。
⾮银流动性⽀持⼯具的要素和操作细节仍有待明确，⽬前有两点相对确定：（ 1 ）主要针对债券
市场。潘功胜⾏⻓指出 “ ⽬的是建⽴债券市场稳定机制，在资管机构⾯临群体性流动性⻛险、债
券市场出现赎回 — 抛售负向循环的特定情景下启⽤ ” 。近些年流动性⻛险和负反馈的典型案例是
2022 年四季度，政策变化带来⻛偏和基本⾯预期上修，债券市场出现了 “ 利率快速上⾏ — 理财净
值回撤 — 客⼾赎回 — 抛售债券 — 利率加速上⾏ — 理财净值进⼀步回撤 ” 的循环。在类似情景下，
央⾏只能通过加⼤对银⾏体系的流动性⽀持，借助 “ 银⾏ — ⾮银 ” 的流动性传递路径来稳定⾮银负

债进⽽稳定市场，政策效果会打折扣，在部分情景下（⽐如信⽤⻛险引发的负反馈）还可能造
成流动性淤积过于宽松，滋⽣资⾦空转套利；⽽设⽴新⼯具后，流动性可直达⾮银机构，增强
政策效⼒同时也避免流动性⼤幅波动；（ 2 ）操作可能是以 “ 互换 ” 形式，主要⾯向公募基⾦、理财
及其对应的证券⺟公司与银⾏⺟公司。依据《中国⼈⺠银⾏法》，央⾏不能直接对⾮银机构发放
贷款。今年 6 ⽉央⾏在陆家嘴论坛上提出研究 “ 机构群体性⾯临流动性危机且可能引发系统性⻛险
时，通过互换⽅式向⾮银机构提供紧急流动性 ” 。
在此次⾦融街论坛上，央⾏指出，这项⼯具的⽬的是建⽴债券市场稳定机制，构成⼈⺠银⾏宏
观审慎管理的⼀项新⼯具。⼯具综合权衡防范⾦融市场体系的系统性⻛险和道德⻛险，在资管
机构⾯临群体性流动性⻛险、债券市场出现赎回 — 抛售负向循环的特定情景时启⽤；与央⾏开
展操作必须满⾜宏观审慎要求、提供⾼等级抵押品，防⽌⾦融市场定价扭曲、形成兜底幻觉，
维护市场纪律。⼯具适⽤的机构包括银⾏理财⼦公司、公募基⾦管理⼈，以及对应的银⾏⺟

⾏、证券⺟公司等。我们将在公开市场业务公告中明确⼯具操作相关要素。

### Page 4

再来看公开市场操作主要品种切换为隔夜品种、政策利率切换为隔夜利率。7 ⽉中旬央⾏曾提出
逐步增加隔夜逆回购操作频次，之后隔夜逆回购操作频次和规模明显升⾼，市场对于进⼀步改
⾰有所预期。 10 ⽉以来 10 年和 30 年国债利率有不同程度的下⾏，其中可能包含对这⼀改⾰趋势
的定价。我们理解，这⼀举措主要⽬的是提⾼市场利率和政策利率期限的匹配度，完善短端利
率调控机制。今年年初以来，央⾏已多次向外传递市场基准利率应该更加关注 DR001 这种隔夜
品种，其成交量占⽐常年在 90% 以上，更能反映货币市场的真实情况，对于政策⽽⾔更短期的
市场基准利率调控难度会更低，海外⼤部分成熟央⾏都锚定隔夜品种；⽽央⾏主要操作品种为 7
天逆回购， DR001 对标的政策利率为 7 天逆回购利率，在期限上明显错配。将公开市场操作品种
切换为隔夜、政策利率切换为隔夜逆回购利率，⼀则有助于提⾼流动性管理的精细度，更好的
“ 削峰填⾕ ” ，提⾼政策对市场利率的掌控⼒；⼆则实现市场利率与政策利率期限匹配，理顺政策

利率传导机制。
据新华⽹报道，今年 7 ⽉中旬，央⾏在国新办新闻发布会上指出，下⼀步，⼈⺠银⾏将继续平稳
有序推动货币政策操作框架的改⾰完善，更好引导市场隔夜利率在政策利率附近平稳运⾏。结
合⼀级交易商需求，研究逐步增加隔夜逆回购的操作频率，并和市场做好沟通。
关于贷款基准利率从单⼀转为多元基准，央⾏在今年⼀季度货币政策专栏中有过国际经验的相
关介绍；今年三季度部分银⾏已有相关实践，发放了利率挂钩 DR001 或 DR007 的贷款。在前期
报告《 26Q1 货政报告的六点增量信息》中，我们对其有过详细解读：主要经济体贷款利率定价
基准多元，⼀个重要原因在于直接融资的发展让企业部⻔能够以更低的成本、更加市场化的利
率来融资，进⽽倒逼贷款利率市场化。海外不少国家案例显⽰，融资渠道多元的⼤型企业的贷
款定价基准均是市场利率，⽽较难发债融资的中⼩型企业与零售客⼾贷款定价基准仍挂钩政策
利率。⽬前我国企业债券融资利率与贷款利率的利差已经明显拉开，直接融资对信贷的替代作
⽤愈发明显；为适应债券利率的下⾏，越来越多的贷款以 LPR 减点的利率来发放，今年上半年

LPR 减点发放的贷款⽐重接近 50% ，明显⾼于 2019 年 LPR 改⾰时的⽔平。为适应直接融资的发
展、保持 LPR 的引导作⽤、促进社会综合融资成本低位运⾏，我国贷款有必要逐步转向多元基准
利率定价。在 LPR 之外，银⾏若再有意愿选择某⼀市场利率作为补充性贷款基准利率，理论上⾄
少需满⾜两个条件：⼀是能充分反映银⾏负债成本，⼆是波动偏低。由此推断，未来政策稳定
货币市场利率、降低波动的必要性会更为凸显。从 6 ⽉陆家嘴论坛⾄此次⾦融街论坛，央⾏创设
隔夜逆回购品种、将隔夜逆回购作为主要操作品种、政策利率切换为隔夜利率、缩窄隐性利率
⾛廊（临时隔夜正逆回购利率区间）等完善短端利率调控举措，与贷款基准利率多元化相辅相
成。
据湖北⽇报报道，今年 7 ⽉招商银⾏向⼀家优质⺠营科技企业发放贷款 2000 万元，期限 1 年。该
笔贷款采⽤定价⽇前 3 个⽉ DR001 均值作为基准利率，并加点 87BP 确定执⾏利率，按 3 个⽉周期
进⾏重定价，实现贷款定价与货币市场利率挂钩，为 DR 基准利率在实体经济融资领域应⽤提供

实践案例。
退出存贷款基准利率更多是完成制度上的收尾、补全利率市场化框架的完整性，如央⾏所指出
的 “ ⾦融机构存贷款基准利率⾃ 2015 年 10 ⽉以来，⻓期未做调整，在存贷款定价中实际上已不再
发挥作⽤，⽬前只剩下在少数司法领域作为计算利息的依据 ” 。在⾦融领域，贷款定价⼤致经历
了三个阶段：（ 1 ） 2013 年之前盯住贷款基准利率；（ 2 ） 2013 年放开贷款利率管制，新定价基准
未形成，处于换锚的过渡期，实际定价中部分银⾏的贷款利率依然会参考贷款基准利率；（ 3 ）
2019 年 LPR 改⾰之后，贷款利率定价锚定 LPR 。后续按照央⾏政策演进脉络，贷款基准可能进
⼀步变为 “LPR+ 市场利率（如 DR ） ” 。存款利率定价变化更加缓慢。央⾏在 2015 年名义上放开存
款利率管制，存款利率定价实际受⾃律机制牵引， 2022 年之后由于贷款利率单边跟随 LPR 和政
策利率调整明显下⾏，存款利率定价调整缓慢，息差压⼒升⾼，央⾏宣布建⽴存款利率调整机
制，存款利率跟随 LPR 和 10 年国债利率调整，⽽ LPR 实际受政策利率（ 7 天逆回购利率）牵引，

所以在 2022 年之后，对于⾦融市场⽽⾔存贷款利率定价实际都统归于政策利率，利率传导机制
为 “ 政策利率 —LPR/10 年国债到期收益率 / 货币市场短端利率 — 存贷款利率与其他债券利率 ” 。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
217 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
在此次⾦融街论坛上，央⾏指出，过去较⻓时间，央⾏直接规定⾦融机构的存贷款基准利率。
随着利率市场化改⾰推进，存贷款基准利率逐步被市场化的定价基准替代。银⾏贷款利率已经
有贷款市场报价利率、银⾏间隔夜回购利率等多个定价基准；⾃今年⼀季度开始，我们对外公
布了各个期限的存款平均利率，作为银⾏存款定价的参考。⾦融机构存贷款基准利率⾃ 2015 年
10 ⽉以来，⻓期未做调整，在存贷款定价中实际上已不再发挥作⽤，⽬前只剩下在少数司法领
域作为计算利息的依据。下⼀步，⼈⺠银⾏将推动这些领域的计息依据⽤市场化的利率指标来
替代，实现退出⾦融机构存贷款基准利率。
综上所述，我们不能简单地从 “ 类降息 ” 等狭义视⻆去理解政策举措，四项举措⼀⽅⾯有助于维护
流动性合理充裕、畅通利率传导；另⼀⽅⾯，它是既定的也是货币政策框架由数量型调控向价

格型调控转型、利率市场化改⾰的延续。关于政策利率与⼴谱利率以及实体融资成本的后续传
导机制和主要特征，仍具有⼀定复杂性，需要进⼀步观察。
10 ⽉以来，流动性明显变松， DR001 中枢降⾄ 1.3% 以下。对于这⼀轮宽松，我们在前期报告
《⾦融数据与流动性环境》中指出有⼀定的政策主导特征，主要⽬的是协同财政稳增⻓，稳定
市场。
最后再看对资产定价的影响。对于债券市场来说，政策操作品种与利率切换、贷款基准多元化
等有助于降低央⾏投放资⾦的负债成本、稳定流动性，同时⼀则前期利率曲线已有初步定价；
⼆则同⽇出来的三季度 GDP 增速略超预期，利率所⾯临的经济基本⾯预期亦会有所上修；三则
从 9 ⽉单⽉固投和消费数据反弹来看，前期⼀揽⼦政策的效果也初步显现，所以要避免形成对利
率定价的单边预期。对于权益资产⽽⾔，前期主要定价全球流动性收敛、逆全球化、 AI 产业链供
需关系等，对于国内政策和基本⾯的定价相对不⾜，⾦融街论坛期间推出的⼏项举措有利于稳
定增⻓条件，顺周期和内需相关资产将得到⼀定⽀撑，所以应告别单线索、适度提升配置⼴谱

性。
本⽂来源：郭磊宏观茶座

Document 496: 世帯年収が低い層のこどもの金融リテラシー形成をどう支えるか ～英国Money and Pensions Service（MaPS）の調査から得られる日本への示唆～

Source PDF: all report pdf/世帯年収が低い層のこどもの金融リテラシー形成をどう支えるか ～英国Money and Pensions Service（MaPS）の調査から得られる日本への示唆～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 13
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年 10 月 5 日

1/13 
世帯年収が低い層のこどもの金融リテラシー形成をどう支えるか 
～英国 Money and Pensions Service（MaPS）の調査から得られる日本への示唆～ 
 資産運用調査部 研究員  小林 菜

（要旨） 
○ 金融リテラシーは、金融に関する知識だけでなく、適切に判断・行動するための技能・態度・
行動を含む。このため、こどもの金融リテラシー形成には、学校等で知識を学ぶだけでなく、
家庭や日常生活のなかで、お金を扱い、考え、判断する経験を積むことが重要である。 
○ 英国Money and Pensions Service（MaPS）の調査を通して示唆されるのは、低所得世帯のこ
どもは、家庭の経済的事情などを背景に、①お小遣いや家事報酬等を通じてお金を扱う経験、
②長期的な家計計画に触れる経験、③家庭内でお金について対話する機会、④保護者から複
雑な金融知識や行動を学ぶ機会が限られる傾向にあるということである。低所得世帯のこど
もの金融リテラシー形成上の課題は、本人の「能力不足」「意欲不足」ではなく、こうした
「経験・機会の不足」という観点から捉える必要がある。 
○ 日本のファイナンシャル・ウェルビーイングに関する実態調査でも、金融リテラシー向上の
ためにこどもへかけてもよいと思う費用や、人生設計・ライフプラン・マネープランの有無、
リスク性資産の購入経験・保有率などに所得階層による違いがみられた。英国の調査結果を
そのまま日本に当てはめることはできないものの、日本においても家庭の経済状況によって
金融リテラシー形成につながる経験や機会に差が生じている可能性がある。 
○ 支援にあたっては、知識提供だけでなく、模擬家計や買い物、給与明細、銀行口座、デジタ
ル決済等を題材とした体験型学習を組み合わせることが重要である。また、保護者への相談
支援、学校・こども食堂・児童館等を通じた学習機会の提供、インターネット等を利用しに
くい世帯も想定した対面・紙媒体での情報提供など、家庭環境に左右されず学べる仕組みが
求められる。 
○ 金融経済教育は、資産形成や投資のための教育にとどまらない。家庭環境による金融経験の
格差を補い、すべてのこどもが生活費管理、契約、信用・借入れ、消費者被害への対処など、
将来の生活を主体的に営むための力を身につける機会を保障するものである。今後は、金融
経済教育を「こどもの貧困・格差対策」の一環としても位置付け、低所得世帯のこどもが直
面する金融リテラシー形成上の障壁について、 日本でも実態把握を進めることが重要である。

### Page 2

2/13 
1. はじめに 
日本では学習指導要領の改訂や資産形成政策を背景に、こども・若者への金融経済教育が拡充
されている（西野 2024; 鄭 2025）。しかし、すべてのこどもが同じ条件で金融リテラシーを形成
できるわけではない。 
本稿では金融リテラシーを、OECD金融教育に関する国際ネットワーク（INFE: International 
Network on Financial Education）の定義に基づき、知識だけでなく、金融面に関して適切に判
断し行動するための意識・技能・態度・行動を含む概念として整理する。同定義では、金融リテ
ラシーを「金融に関する健全な意思決定を行い、究極的には金融面での個人の良い暮らし（well-
being）を達成するために必要な金融に関する意識、知識、技術、態度及び行動の総体」としてい
る（OECD/INFE 2012; 金融庁 2013）。 
このように、より良い暮らしを達成するために必要な金融リテラシーが、知識だけでなく技術
や行動も含む限り、こどもたちにとっては、授業で伝えられる知識だけでは十分ではなく、 「学ん
だ内容を実践・定着させることのできる環境」が重要になる。 
しかしながら、本稿の議論を先取りして言うならば、世帯年収が低い層（以下、 「低所得世
帯」 ）のこどもたちは、知識の伝達に加えて、 「学んだ内容を実践・定着させることのできる環
境」について、様々な障壁を抱えやすい。具体的には、家庭の経済的事情から、お小遣いを使っ
た実践の機会や、お金に関する家庭内の会話機会、長期的な家計計画に触れる機会など、金融リ
テラシーを身につける経験そのものが限られやすい傾向がある。 
日本では2024 年時点で、こどもの貧困率は 11.0％、こどもがいる現役世帯のうち「大人が一
人」の世帯の貧困率は44.7％に達する（厚生労働省 2026）。低所得世帯に暮らすこどもたちの金
融リテラシー形成をどう支えるかという問題は、重要なテーマといえる。 
そこで本稿では、英国のMoney and Pensions Service（以下、MaPS）の調査報告書「Children 
& Young People living in low-income households and their financial education: Research 
findings」 （以下、 「MaPS調査」 ）と、ウェルビーイング学会ファイナンシャル・ウェルビーイング
分科会による「ファイナンシャル・ウェルビーイングに関する実態調査」 （以下、 「FWB調査」 ）を
手掛かりに、低所得世帯のこどもに対する支援の必要性と方向性を検討する。なお、本稿におけ
る議論では、初等中等教育期のこどもたちを主な対象として想定している。

2. MaPS調査とFWB調査の概要 
英国のMaPSは、英国全土の人々が生涯にわたって効果的な金融上の意思決定を行うために必要
な指針や情報にアクセスできるよう支援を行う、労働年金省（Department for Work and 
Pensions）所管の公的機関である。MaPS調査は、2025 年に、英国の低所得世帯に暮らす11～18歳
のこども・若者58名、その保護者・養育者58名、支援実務者40 名を対象として行われた定性調
査である。当該調査では、低所得世帯のこどもの経験が、金融リテラシー形成にどのような影響
を及ぼすかを検討している。 
他方で日本では、筆者が確認した範囲では、低所得世帯に暮らすこどもたちの経験と金融リテ

### Page 3

3/13 
ラシー形成との関連に焦点を当てた詳細な調査は限定的である。英国の結果をそのまま日本に当
てはめることはできないものの、日本の低所得世帯のこどもたちの金融リテラシー形成支援のあ
り方を考えるうえで、当該調査の結果は貴重な参照資料といえる。 
本稿で用いるもう一つの調査―FWB調査―は、ウェルビーイング学会ファイナンシャル・ウェル
ビーイング分科会によって2025年 1 月に日本で実施された。調査対象者は全国の18～79歳であ
り、Webアンケートを用いて実施された。サンプルサイズは11,022人である。なお、FWB調査は成
人を対象としており、こどもの経験を直接測定するものではないが、家庭環境に所得差があるこ
とによる「こどもの機会格差」を考える材料として取り扱っている。

3. MaPS調査・FWB調査から示唆される、低所得世帯のこどもが直面する 4 つの「機会不足」 
MaPS調査とFWB調査からは、低所得世帯のこどもたちが、家庭の経済的事情から様々な点で金融
リテラシーを身につける経験そのものが限られやすいことが示唆されている。

(1)お金を扱う経験の不足 
MaPS調査によれば、低所得世帯のこどもたちの金銭に関する学びは、主に家庭の状況や、お小
遣いをもらったり家事を手伝って報酬をもらったりするといった非公式な経験、年長の若者につ
いてはアルバイトの経験などによって形作られている。他方で、親が月末に金銭的に苦しい状況
にある場合はお小遣いの支給が遅れたり、減額されたりすることがあるという。また、特にひと
り親家庭では、定期的なお小遣いをもらっていると報告するこどもの割合は低かったという。 
このように、経済的余裕が乏しい家庭では、お小遣いや家事報酬を渡すこと自体が難しい場合
があると考えられる。 
家庭における金融経済教育の方法として、あんびる（2024）は、 「家計管理はお小遣いなど限ら
れたお金を題材にして小さいうちからまず取り組むべきもの」 「支出の意味（自分の心のどのよう
なニーズを充足させたのか）を問うことで、自分にとって生活に必要なものとは何かを知る」こ
とができる、 「お小遣いから貯蓄をし、必要な時に使う経験は、貯蓄の意義の理解を促し、生活設
計、資産形成の基礎」となると指摘している。こどもたちにとってお小遣いは、家計管理やニー
ズとウォンツの区別といった金融リテラシーに直結する経験を積むための最も重要なツールであ
る。しかしながらMaPSの調査結果からは、低所得世帯で暮らすこどもたちは経済的困窮により、
お小遣いや家事報酬を用いて「実際に予算を立てる」「使い道を選ぶ」「貯める」「失敗から学ぶ」
といった金融リテラシー形成のための実践機会が失われている可能性が示唆されている。 
日本におけるFWB調査でも、保護者がこどもの金融リテラシー向上にかけてもよいと思う費用
（金融リテラシー向上のための習い事、資格取得、そのための学習などを含む）には、所得階層
による違いがみられる。保護者に対し、 「あなたのお子様（未成年）の金融リテラシーを高めるた
めにかけてもいいと思う月額の費用をお答えください」（金融リテラシー向上のための習い事、資
格取得、そのための学習などを含む）と尋ねた結果、月5,000円以上の金額をかけてもよいと回
答した人の割合は、等価世帯年収（注 1）の低位群で22.7％、中位群で28.1％、高位群で30.3％

### Page 4

4/13 
となり、所得階層が高いほど高い傾向がみられた。

究所作成

(2)長期的な計画に触れる機会の不足 
MaPS調査によれば、低所得世帯の一部の家庭では、請求書の支払いや生活必需品の購入といっ
た差し迫った必要性に追われすぎて、長期的な財務計画や教育に注力する余裕がないという。 
このように、低所得世帯では、請求書や生活必需品など目前の支払いへの対応が優先されやす
いことから、保護者が長期的なライフプランやマネープランを立てる余裕を持ちにくく、こども
も計画的で長期的な家計運営を身近に見たり学んだりする機会が少なくなると考えられる。 
 実際に日本におけるFWB調査では、長期的な計画（人生設計、ライフプラン、マネープラン）の
有無には、世帯の所得階層による違いがみられる。具体的には、人生設計、ライフプラン、マネ
ープランのいずれについても、所得階層が高いほど「計画がある」と回答した人の割合が高い傾
向がみられた。例えば、人生設計を立てている人の割合は低位群17.2％、中位群24.4％、高位群
30.5％であり、マネープランについても低位群15.3％、中位群23.2％、高位群28.0％となってい
る。
